A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def gridGame(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        result = float("inf")
        left = 0
        right = sum(grid[0])

        for top, bottom in zip(grid[0], grid[1]):
            right -= top
            result = min(result, max(left, right))
            left += bottom

        return result
```

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def gridGame(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        result = float("inf")
        left, right = 0, sum(grid[0])

        for top, bottom in zip(grid[0], grid[1]):
            right -= top
            result = min(result, max(left, right))
            left += bottom

        return result
```

Let's maybe verify with known LeetCode 2017 official solution:
```
class Solution:
    def gridGame(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        ans = inf
        top = sum(grid[0])
        bottom = 0
        for i in range(len(grid[0])):
            top -= grid[0][i]
            ans = min(ans, max(top, bottom))
            bottom += grid[1][i]
        return ans
```

Let's derive with first robot path switch at column i. Suppose first robot switches at column i, but it might also choose to move down at column i after collecting top[i]. It collects bottom[i] and then bottom[i+1..n-1]. So bottom[i..n-1] collected. The second robot, if it switches down at column j, collects top[0..j] and bottom[j..n-1]. The uncollected cells on its path:
- If j < i: top[0..j] collected by first (yes), bottom[j..i-1] not collected (because first only collected bottom[i..]), bottom[i..n-1] collected. So sum bottom[j..i-1]. Since nonnegative, max at j=0 -> sum bottom[0..i-1].
- If j = i: top[0..i] collected, bottom[i..n-1] collected, sum 0.
- If j > i: top[0..i] collected, top[i+1..j] not collected, bottom[j..n-1] collected. Sum top[i+1..j]. Max at j=n-1 -> sum top[i+1..n-1].
Thus max of two. Good.

Could there be a path where second robot moves down at column j and then up? No, only down once, no up. Good.

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def gridGame(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        result = float("inf")
        left, right = 0, sum(grid[0])

        for top, bottom in zip(grid[0], grid[1]):
            right -= top
            result = min(result, max(left, right))
            left += bottom

        return result

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def gridGame(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        result = float("inf")
        left, right = 0, sum(grid[0])

        for i in range(len(grid[0])):
            right -= grid[0][i]
            result = min(result, max(left, right))
            left += grid[1][i]

        return result
```

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def gridGame(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        result = float("inf")
        left, right = 0, sum(grid[0])

        for i in range(len(grid[0])):
            right -= grid[0][i]
            result = min(result, max(left, right))
            left += grid[1][i]

        return result
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def gridGame(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        result = float("inf")
        left, right = 0, sum(grid[0])

        for i in range(len(grid[0])):
            right -= grid[0][i]
            result = min(result, max(left, right))
            left += grid[1][i]

        return result
